## Codex triage guardrail
- Durable Fable/Opus rulings must not live only in chat.
- If Fable/Opus already recorded the issue, Codex verifies the pointers and leaves it alone.
- If a durable issue is missing, Codex adds the smallest note to the right experiment artifact (exhibit, ambiguity register, O12 bundle, manifest, census binding, or this board).
- FinalDesign docs stay untouched unless owner/Fable explicitly ratifies a back-port.
- Gold keys are Fable-owned: Sonnet/strong may draft key labels, but the model arms being tested (for example Haiku as cheap blind Driver-name producer) never define the gold; Fable adjudicates every record and signs the lock file before any scorer uses it.
- Prompt-finalization preference: when asking Fable to finalize prompts, pass owner preferences as guidance, not a rigid template. Prompts should work for weaker/cheap models; stay broad, clean, minimal, and precise; use examples only when they sharpen a boundary or prevent a common mistake; keep examples few, illustrative, and non-exhaustive; include a light self-check only where useful.

**Judged recall / precision (n=1,175 gold; 60-sample precision):** haiku 31.0% / 81.7% · **sonnet 40.4% / 85.0%** · opus 42.4% / 88.3% · sonnet-para 48.3% / 85.0% · opus-para 51.3% / 93.3% · 2u-union 42.6% / 85.0% · 3u-union 48.6% / 78.3% · ABLATED 57.5% / **18.3%**.

**The three pre-registered adoption bars (verbatim):**
1. **Cheap reader: ADOPT sonnet@high.** Gap to opus = 1.96pts ≤ 2pts (borderline — recorded); precision within Wilson noise. Haiku FAILED by 11.4pts → O16 trigger fired; **Fable DECLINES the DeepSeek fallback arm** (Sonnet already qualifies as the cheaper tier; a fallback can't change any agent()-only downstream; memo in decision.json).
2. **Para-8k: REJECT** per bar — recall/precision non-inferior (para actually +7.9pts recall) but cost RISES 4.6× (183 vs 40 calls); the bar requires a cost drop. Finding recorded: single-pass long-context attention, not missing context, drives misses — relevant to the X-C chunking experiment.
3. **Multi-run union: REJECT both** — 2u gain 2.2pts < 5pts; 3u gain 8.2pts ≥ 5 but new-run junk 36.7% ≫ the 15% single-run bar.

**Ablation:** the inlined rulebook is doing decisive work — without it precision collapses 85.0% → 18.3% (the ablated arm's higher raw recall is name-spray, not skill).

**Attribution + O5:** 251/1,175 gold causes (21.4%) missed by ALL full-rules arms — recorded as `exhibits/ra_exp2_shared_misses.json`; consistent with union-built key density (bars are relative by design). NO rerun authorized (none has a Fable-named fix).

**2026-07-28 STAGING-GATE BLIND SPOTS CLOSED — both derived from ONE staged-file set, both attacked; harness 171 -> 174.** The reviewer found the gate that polices the whole staged effort had two holes, and BOTH were confirmed by running the OLD logic against the attack before anything changed. (1) A LIVE IMPORT OF STAGED CODE WENT UNSEEN: the check compared live sources against a HAND-WRITTEN `("prepared_fact_v2", "slot_convert", "fact_match")`, so when `xbrl_attach.py` became the fourth staged module the check simply stopped covering it — `from driver.core.xbrl_attach import attach_event_xbrl` in a live module: OLD = NOT CAUGHT, NEW = caught. The irony is exact: a gate whose purpose is catching hand-maintained drift was itself a hand-maintained list. (2) EVERY UNTRACKED PRODUCTION FILE WAS IGNORED: `modified` filtered `if not ln.startswith("??")`, so a brand-new production module that was simply never `git add`ed was invisible — `?? driver/core/backdoor.py`: OLD = NOT CAUGHT, NEW = caught. FIX: ONE `STAGED_FILES` set with two pure helpers DERIVED from it — `unexpected_production_files` (untracked production files must BE the staged set; modified ones must be individually allowlisted; tests excluded because the assertion says production) and `live_modules_importing_staged` (module names derived, never re-listed). Both are pure functions taking crafted input, which is what makes them attackable. THREE MUTATION TESTS now prove the attacks fail, each with NEGATIVE CONTROLS so they cannot pass by flagging everything: a live import of EACH of the four staged modules is caught while an ordinary `driver_ids` import is not; two unexpected untracked production files are caught while the four staged files, an untracked TEST file and an allowlisted modification are not; and an un-allowlisted modification is still caught. **A CLAIM OF MINE NARROWED, as instructed:** I had written that "every source id used anywhere in the existing fixtures" was verified lawful. A grep for ONE literal spelling cannot support "every" — it says nothing about ids built dynamically or passed positionally. The v1 suite now names FIVE EXPLICIT CONTROLS and says so, with the full regression standing as the broader evidence. Both the test docstring and the allowlist comment were corrected. **2026-07-28 #822 DONE (owner GO, RED-first) — order-independent row and dimension conflicts; 1041 -> 1049. STOPPING for review before #823.** REPRODUCED FIRST, exactly as recorded: two rows matching the same claim and differing ONLY in a dimension LABEL gave `[Foo, Bar] -> ACCEPTED` and `[Bar, Foo] -> SchemaError`. ROOT: the conflict signature was `tuple(str(r.get(k)) for k in _REQUIRED_ROW_KEYS)` — six hand-named fields that omitted BOTH the period and the dimensions entirely. So the two rows looked identical, collapsed, and `matched_pairs[0]` let the reader's return order pick which LABEL `check_member_refs` recomputed the slice token from. FIX (a): `_row_signature` is DERIVED from `_ROW_FIELDS` — the same union that already declares what binding reads — so a new row field cannot be silently left out of the conflict identity, and no second signature list is hand-maintained. Dimensions render SORTED as `(axis, member, label)` triples, because two readings of one fact listing the same dimensions in a different order are the SAME fact, not a conflict. FIX (b): the adapter's `found[rec["id"]] = rec` was LAST-WRITE-WINS, so which axis or member a fact bound against depended on the order the driver returned rows in; `resolve_id_records` now GROUPS — identical duplicates collapse, and any disagreement on qname or label POISONS the id to `None`, which the existing consumer already treats as an unresolvable pair and drops fail-closed. That is exactly the poisoning the inline binder already applies to duplicate context and unit ids, so the idea is reused rather than invented. PROOFS, permutation-based rather than example-based: both orders of the differing-label pair now PARK (`itertools.permutations`, not two hand-written cases); every `_ROW_FIELDS` member is shown to CHANGE the signature, so the coverage claim is derived not asserted; dimension order proven irrelevant; and THREE POSITIVE CONTROLS hold — identical complete rows still collapse in every order, a single lawful dimensional row still attaches, and one poisoned id does not poison its neighbours. Adapter grouping tested on both orders of three conflict shapes (label differs, qname differs) plus the collapse and neighbour controls. **2026-07-28 #822 REMAINDER — five defects + one of my own test holes; 1049 -> 1063. #823 STILL HELD.** All reproduced before any change. **(1)+(2) LAWFUL EQUIVALENCE READ AS CONFLICT.** My new signature compared RAW values while BINDING compares NORMALISED ones, so two spellings of ONE fact conflicted — the same order-dependence pointing the other way. Each normalisation is now justified by what binding itself does, never a new rule: `fact_id` blank forms collapse because `bind_graph_fact` chooses its path on `(id or "").strip()`; an INSTANT's `end_date` leaves the identity entirely because `match_xbrl_fact` reads only `start_date` there (and the graph stores the literal string `"null"` in all 3,058 instants); and the value compares through the certified `parse_raw`, keeping its RAW text when unreadable so two different unreadable strings never collapse into one. **A THIRD EQUIVALENCE HE DID NOT LIST, found by sweeping the class: comma formatting** — `"726,000,000"` and `"726000000"` are one number, and 807,132 of 1,000,000 live values carry commas, making it the highest-traffic of the three. **(3) A REPEATED DIMENSION AXIS reached graph I/O:** the claim's dimension set is built as a SET of `(axis, member)` pairs, which HIDES the repetition, so an impossible claim (one context carries at most one member per axis) bought a representation read before failing to match. It happened to behave — a repeated-axis row returns None, which never equals a set — which is exactly why it had to go: correct by accident is not correct. The defect is LOST EVIDENCE, not wrong evidence — a structural span cannot have it, because the cell's extent is recorded when the text is walked. I did not manufacture a failing same-label-twice test to match the brief; it is saved as a CONTROL with its green-under-both status stated in the test itself, and the genuinely RED case is saved beside it. **THE CHANGE, and it is as small as he predicted.** `inline_html` keeps the selected cell NODE instead of only its text and records `ev['row_label_span']` from `node_spans` — the same (text, span) pairing `_aligned_columns` already returns for headers ten lines below; `locator` reads that span instead of searching. Correcting it properly would mean making the label TEXT the exact prepared-text slice — which is what the audit already demands for pieces ("does not store a stripped or prettified string beside the original span") — but `raw_label_or_claim` is a PUBLIC packet field and this round was scoped to no public shape change, so it is flagged for the owner, not quietly changed. **2026-07-29 #825 PART-1 REPAIRS — all four reviewer gaps closed; 1358 -> 1371 passed / 1 skipped. ONE was a real production defect worse than the one #828 set out to fix.** **(1) THE FALSEY-ARRAY DEFECT — the sharpest, and it was mine.** `r["dus"] or []` turned EVERY falsey value — `""`, `0`, `False`, `{}`, `()`, `set()` — into an empty list, so a BROKEN dimension array became "this fact has no dimensions" and the row was ACCEPTED. Now frozen at every level, tested at every level (container, row, nested list, nested mapping). It does NOT import the staged deep-freezer: a LIVE module importing staged code is exactly what the G18 gate forbids, so the constructor completes itself over the one shape it owns — the #823 lesson, freeze where the value is built — and the adapter still contains ZERO references to staged modules. The two become one owner at the switch. **(3) V1 LOST THE EXCLUSIONS IT HAD JUST COLLECTED.** `if menu_tokens is not None:` gated the WHOLE `member_menu`, so a run with no slice menu gathered the adapter's exclusions into `menu_logs` and then discarded them — precisely the second silent-drop gate the plan forbids, added by me in the same round that made them visible. Now emitted whenever a menu ran OR anything was excluded, with a completely clean no-menu run still emitting nothing so a quiet event stays quiet. Both halves pinned. **(4) THE POSITIVE CONTROL NEVER CALLED THE OWNER.** It asserted `"frozenset" in getsource(build_menu)` and then passed a hand-written literal — while its own docstring claimed it was not one. It now CALLS `build_menu`, asserts the owner produced a non-empty token set, and passes that through the public door; the source inspection is deleted. **TWO OF MY OWN MIGRATION SLIPS, both caught by the suite:** freezing the rows broke 17 assertions still written against lists (corrected to the frozen shape — the shape IS the repair), and my `assert x == []` regex over-reached onto `get_prior_guide_units`, an unrelated reader that still returns a list — the crude-pattern habit, this time inside a migration script; reverted. **2026-07-29 #825 PART-1 PROOF GAPS CLOSED — test-only; 1371 -> 1376 passed / 1 skipped. No production change.** **(1) ISOLATION, which freezing alone never proved.** The immutability tests showed the RETURNED object cannot be edited. They said nothing about the other half: whether the raw rows, their dimension arrays, or the definition records the adapter read can still be edited AFTERWARDS and move the "verified" result underneath whoever holds it. The new control mutates all three after return — rewrites the raw row's value and context, appends to the `dus`/`mus` lists in place, rewrites and extends the definition records — and asserts the result is byte-for-byte what it was. It passes: no aliasing path exists. **AND I RECORDED WHY IT PASSES, so a later reader is not misled** — every value copied out is an IMMUTABLE SCALAR and `dims` is rebuilt, so there is nothing to share today; the guard exists for the future refactor that shares one. **HONEST LIMIT OF THE TEETH PROOF:** aliasing the row to the raw record, and aliasing a dim to the definition record, both make the test FAIL, so it is not vacuous — but both trip the shape assertions before reaching the isolation comparison, so what is proved is "not vacuous", not "the isolation comparison itself is load-bearing". My first mutation attempt was worse than that: it was a NO-OP that left the code equivalent and reported a green pass, which would have been a fake teeth proof had I not checked what it actually changed. **(2) THE OLD MALFORMED-ARRAY TEST HAD WEAKENED TO NOTHING.** After the frozen-shape migration it asserted only `isinstance(.rows, tuple)` — true of EVERY possible outcome, including keeping the bad row, so it could not fail. Its six cases are MERGED into the strong matrix (now ten, adding the falsey ones), each asserting zero returned rows, exactly one `graph_row_unreadable`, and exact fact/context/concept values. The old test is DELETED rather than left beside its replacement, so there is one owner for the claim. **2026-07-29 #825 PART 2 — production BUILT and proven (11/11); the test estate is RED at 248 and I am stopping rather than bulk-fixing it.** BUILT AND GREEN ON ITS OWN TESTS: `AttachResult(source_id, facts, preflight_outcomes, member_menu)` — one immutable event result, frozen at every level, carrying `source_id` WITH the facts so a verified event cannot be paired with a different one at handoff. The pure phase now runs PER ITEM with the original index carried from `enumerate` and never renumbered, so a malformed item becomes its own rejection row instead of aborting the comprehension and erasing valid siblings — the actual contract defect. Event-wide causes (conflicting hashes, representation count, provider outage, missing company) FAN OUT to every still-valid item, each keeping its index; a concept-level absence parks ONLY the items claiming that concept; a programming error is never converted into an item row and still aborts loudly. Outcomes use the CLI's exact five fields, frozen, with the five public decision words only — `parked_retry` stays an EXCEPTION CLASS, never a sixth decision. The three defaults are pinned (`SchemaError`→rejected/XBRL_CONTRACT_INVALID · `ProductionValidationError`→parked/XBRL_BINDING_UNAVAILABLE · `SourceUnavailable`→parked/SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE), `SlotConversionError` keeps its own `NOT_STORABLE`, and the item-class tuple is DERIVED from `OUTCOME_CLASSES` so a fifth class cannot be added there and silently escape here. Nothing reads an exception message to choose. **WHY I STOPPED.** The call-site migration is mechanical for ~66 of the failures (`.facts` / `.facts[0][1]` shapes, 12 files already patched) but **182 of the 248 are `DID NOT RAISE`** — tests asserting the OLD law, that a single-item event whose only item fails RAISES. Under part 2 an item-local failure no longer raises; it returns as an outcome row, while an envelope-level failure still raises. Deciding which each of those 182 is — envelope or item — is EXACTLY the per-test judgement the #824 review forbade me from bulk-changing, and doing it mechanically would convert real coverage into whatever makes the suite green. That is a fresh-context job, and I will not start it on the tail of this one. **CURRENT STATE, stated plainly: the tree is RED at 248 failed / 1143 passed.** The production change is complete and self-proven; the estate migration is not. Nothing is committed. HELD: #826, atomic switch, Fiscal changes, AI calls, graph writes, commit, push.
